This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."
  ]

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

Infection occurs by inhaling contaminated water droplets, primarily affecting older or health-impaired individuals. The report reminds building and water system operators to maintain and monitor their systems. Read more: [https://lnkd.in/gQ5537jS](https://www.linkedin.com/redir/redirect?url=https%3A%2F%2Flnkd%2Ein%2FgQ5537jS&urlhash=q_rH&trk=public_post-text)[#ERISInfo](https://www.linkedin.com/signup/cold-join?session_redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.linkedin.com%2Ffeed%2Fhashtag%2Ferisinfo&trk=public_post-text)[#ERISInsider](https://www.linkedin.com/signup/cold-join?session_redirect=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.linkedin.com%2Ffeed%2Fhashtag%2Ferisinsider&trk=public_post-text)[Public Health Ontario](https://ca.linkedin.com/company/public-health-ontario?trk=public_post-text)[Public Health Agency of Canada | Agence de la santé publique du Canada](https://ca.linkedin.com/company/public-health-agency-of-canada?trk=public_post-text)"
  ]

    "## Secondary Menu [Maps & Directions](https://www.lhsc.on.ca/patients-visitors/finding-your-way-around-lhsc) ![London Health Sciences Centre](/sites/default/files/LHSC_Horizontal_Pride_Logo_0.png \"London Health Sciences Centre Home\") ## Main navigation ## Breadcrumb # Media Statement - Positive result for Legionella bacteria at London Health Sciences Centre July 23, 2025 The Middlesex-London Health Unit (MLHU) has declared an [outbreak of Legionnaires’ disease in the London area](https://www.healthunit.com/legionnaires-disease). This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."
  ]
}

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

    "## Secondary Menu [Maps & Directions](https://www.lhsc.on.ca/patients-visitors/finding-your-way-around-lhsc) ![London Health Sciences Centre](/sites/default/files/LHSC_Horizontal_Pride_Logo_0.png \"London Health Sciences Centre Home\") ## Main navigation ## Breadcrumb # Media Statement - Positive result for Legionella bacteria at London Health Sciences Centre July 23, 2025 The Middlesex-London Health Unit (MLHU) has declared an [outbreak of Legionnaires’ disease in the London area](https://www.healthunit.com/legionnaires-disease). This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."

More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."
  ]
}

This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. The [likely source](https://www.cbc.ca/news/health/legionnaires-outbreak-london-source-1.7621817) of the outbreak was the [cooling towers of a local meat-processing plant](https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/london/legionnaires-outbreak-london-likely-source-1.7618150) in the city's east end, local health officials said. A recent outbreak in [New York City](https://apnews.com/article/legionnaires-disease-new-york-harlem-e509d666283abb9e22492a374c62c9f5), meanwhile, killed seven people and sickened dozens more. Health officials in that city link the Central Harlem outbreak to cooling towers — structures containing water and a fan that are used to cool buildings.",
    "## Secondary Menu [Maps & Directions](https://www.lhsc.on.ca/patients-visitors/finding-your-way-around-lhsc) ![London Health Sciences Centre](/sites/default/files/LHSC_Horizontal_Pride_Logo_0.png \"London Health Sciences Centre Home\") ## Main navigation ## Breadcrumb # Media Statement - Positive result for Legionella bacteria at London Health Sciences Centre July 23, 2025 The Middlesex-London Health Unit (MLHU) has declared an [outbreak of Legionnaires’ disease in the London area](https://www.healthunit.com/legionnaires-disease). This is a serious type of pneumonia caused by Legionella bacteria, which people can get by breathing in mist or vapour from contaminated water sources. The bacteria can grow in places like hot tubs, cooling towers, plumbing systems, fountains, and parts of air conditioning systems in large buildings. It does not spread from person to person. Those most at risk include people over 65, individuals with weakened immune systems, and those with chronic lung conditions. ### **Legionnaires’ disease and LHSC** The MLHU has confirmed that LHSC is within the [six-kilometre area where most local cases of Legionnaires’ disease have been found](https://www.healthunit.com/confirmed-legionnaires-disease-cases). On July 16 and 17 samples were taken from Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers and air-conditioning systems. Yesterday, we learned that Victoria Hospital’s cooling towers tested positive for Legionella bacteria on an initial screening test. More testing is now underway to find out if it matches the strain causing the community outbreak. Those results will take approximately three weeks. For now, after discussions with MLHU clinical leadership, we want to be clear: this does **not** mean Victoria Hospital is the source of the community outbreak."
  ]
